69→\n    70→sort_select_mthds(Clients, undefined, Servers) ->\n    71→    %% User has not expressed an opinion via option \"auth_methods\", use the server's prefs\n    72→ 80→sort_select_mthds1(Clients, Users0, Servers0) ->\n    81→    Servers = unique(Servers0),\n    82→    Users = unique(Users0),\n    83→    [C || Key <- Users,\n    84→\t  lists:member(Key, 76→%% Description: Handles channel messages received on the ssh-connection.\n    77→%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n    78→handle_ssh_msg({ssh_cm, _ConnectionHandler,\n    79→\t\t{data, _ChannelId, _Type, Data}}, \n    80→\t       #state{group = Group} = State0) ->\n    81→    {Enc, State} = guess_encoding(Data, State0),\n    82→    List = 100→\t\t\t\t success, ChannelId),\n   101→    {ok, State};\n   102→\n   103→handle_ssh_msg({ssh_cm, ConnectionHandler,\n   104→\t    {env, ChannelId, WantReply, Var, Value}}, State = #state{encoding=Enc0}) ->\n   105→    %% It is not as simple as it sounds to set environment variables\n   106→    %% in the server.\n   107→    %% The (OS) env vars should be per per Channel; otherwise anyone\n   108→    %% could affect anyone other's variables.\n   109→    %% Therefore this clause always return a failure.\n   110→    ssh_connection:reply_request(ConnectionHandler,\n   111→\t\t\t\t WantReply, failure, ChannelId),\n   112→\n   113→    %% https://pubs.opengroup.org/onlinepubs/7908799/xbd/envvar.html\n   114→    %% LANG\n   115→    %%     This variable determines the locale category for native language,\n   116→    %%     local customs and coded character set in the absence of the LC_ALL\n   117→    %%     and other LC_* (LC_COLLATE, LC_CTYPE, LC_MESSAGES, LC_MONETARY,\n   118→    %%     LC_NUMERIC, LC_TIME) environment variables. This can be used by\n   119→    %%     applications to determine the language to use for error messages\n   120→    %%     and instructions, collating sequences, date formats, and so forth. \n   121→    %% LC_ALL\n   122→    %%     This variable determines the values for all locale categories. The\n   123→    %%     value of the LC_ALL environment variable has precedence over any of\n   124→    %%     the other environment variables starting with LC_ (LC_COLLATE,\n   125→    %%     LC_CTYPE, LC_MESSAGES, LC_MONETARY, LC_NUMERIC, LC_TIME) and the\n   126→    %%     LANG environment variable.\n   127→    %% ...\n   128→    %%\n   129→    %%  The values of locale categories are determined by a precedence order;\n   130→    %%  the first condition met below determines the value:\n   131→    %%\n   132→    %%   1. If the LC_ALL environment variable is defined and is not null,\n   133→    %%      the value of LC_ALL is used.\n   134→    %%\n   135→    %%   2. If the LC_* environment variable ( LC_COLLATE, LC_CTYPE, LC_MESSAGES,\n   136→    %%      LC_MONETARY, LC_NUMERIC, LC_TIME) is defined and is not null, the\n   137→    %%      value of the environment variable is used to initialise the category\n   138→    %%      that corresponds to the environment variable.\n   139→    %%\n   140→    %%   3. If the LANG environment variable is defined and is not null, the value\n   141→    %%      of the LANG environment variable is used.\n   142→    %%\n   143→    %%   4. If the LANG environment variable is not set or is set to the empty string,\n   144→    %%      the implementation-dependent default locale is used. \n   145→\n   146→    Enc =\n   147→        %% Rule 1 and 3 above says that LC_ALL has precedence over LANG. The term\n   762→in the return value is formatted to a string if it is a non-string type. No\n   763→trailing newline is added in the ok-case.\n   764→\n   765→See the User's Guide section on\n   766→[One-Time Execution](using_ssh.md#one-time-execution) for examples.\n   767→\n   768→Error texts are returned on channel-type 1 which usually is piped to `stderr` on\n   769→e.g Linux systems. Texts from a successful execution are returned on\n   770→channel-type 0 and will in similar manner be piped to `stdout`. The exit-status\n   771→code is set to 0 for success and 255 for errors. The exact results presented on\n   772→the client side depends on the client and the client's operating system.\n   773→\n   774→In case of the `{direct, exec_fun()}` variant or no exec-option at all, all\n   775→reads from `standard_input` will be from the received data-events of type 0.\n   776→Those are sent by the client. Similarly all writes to `standard_output` will be\n   777→sent as data-events to the client. An OS shell client like the command 'ssh'\n   778→will usually use stdin and stdout for the user interface.\n   779→\n   780→The option cooperates with the daemon-option\n   781→[`shell`](`t:shell_daemon_option/0`) in the following way:\n   782→\n   783→- **1\\. If neither the [`exec-option`](`t:exec_daemon_option/0`) nor the\n   784→  [`shell-option`](`t:shell_daemon_option/0`) is present:** - The default Erlang\n   785→  evaluator is used both for exec and shell requests. The result is returned to\n   786→  the client.\n   787→\n   788→- **2\\. If the [`exec_spec`](`t:exec_daemon_option/0`)'s value is `disabled`\n   789→  (the [`shell-option`](`t:shell_daemon_option/0`) may or may not be\n   790→  present):** - No exec-requests are executed but shell-requests are not\n   791→  affected, they follow the [`shell_spec`](`t:shell_daemon_option/0`)'s value.\n   792→\n   793→- **3\\. If the [`exec-option`](`t:exec_daemon_option/0`) is present and the\n   794→  [`exec_spec`](`t:exec_daemon_option/0`) value =/= `disabled` (the\n   795→  [`shell-option`](`t:shell_daemon_option/0`) may or may not be present):** -\n   796→  The [`exec_spec`](`t:exec_daemon_option/0`) `fun()` is called with the same\n   797→  number of parameters as the arity of the fun, and the result is returned to\n   798→  the client. Shell-requests are not affected, they follow the\n   799→  [`shell_spec`](`t:shell_daemon_option/0`)'s value.\n   800→\n   801→- **4\\. If the [`exec-option`](`t:exec_daemon_option/0`) is absent, and the\n   802→  [`shell-option`](`t:shell_daemon_option/0`) is present with the default Erlang\n   803→  shell as the [`shell_spec`](`t:shell_daemon_option/0`)'s value:** - The\n   804→  default Erlang evaluator is used both for exec and shell requests. The result\n   805→  is returned to the client.\n   806→\n   807→- **5\\. If the [`exec-option`](`t:exec_daemon_option/0`) is absent, and the\n   808→  [`shell-option`](`t:shell_daemon_option/0`) is present with a value that is\n   809→  neither the default Erlang shell nor the value `disabled`:** - The\n   810→  exec-request is not evaluated and an error message is returned to the client.\n   811→  Shell-requests are executed according to the value of the\n   812→  [`shell_spec`](`t:shell_daemon_option/0`).\n   813→\n   814→- **6\\. If the [`exec-option`](`t:exec_daemon_option/0`) is absent, and the\n   815→  [`shell_spec`](`t:shell_daemon_option/0`)'s value is `disabled`:** - Exec\n   816→  requests are executed by the default shell, but shell-requests are not\n   817→  executed.\n   818→\n   819→If a custom CLI is installed (see the option\n   820→[`ssh_cli`](`t:ssh_cli_daemon_option/0`)) the rules above are replaced by thoose\n   821→implied by the custom CLI.\n   822→\n   823→> #### Note {: .info }\n   824→>\n   825→> The [`exec-option`](`t:exec_daemon_option/0`) has existed for a long time but\n   826→> has not previously been documented. The old definition and behaviour are\n   827→> retained but obey the rules 1-6 above if conflicting. The old and undocumented\n   828→> style should not be used in new programs.\n   829→\"\"\".\n   830→-doc(#{title => <<\"Daemon Options\">>}).\n   831→-type exec_result()  :: {ok,Result::term()} | {error,Reason::term()} .\n   832→-doc \"\"\"\n   833→Old-style exec specification that are kept for compatibility, but should not be\n   834→used in new programs\n   835→\"\"\".\n   836→-doc(#{title => <<\"Daemon Options\">>}).\n   837→-type deprecated_exec_opt() :: fun() | mod_fun_args() .\n   838→\n   839→-doc \"\"\"\n   840→Provides your own CLI implementation in a daemon.\n   841→\n   842→It is a channel callback module that implements a shell and command execution.\n   843→The shell's read-eval-print loop can be customized, using the option\n   844→[`shell`](`t:shell_daemon_option/0`). This means less work than implementing an\n   845→own CLI channel. If `ssh_cli` is set to `no_cli`, the CLI channels like\n   846→[`shell`](`t:shell_daemon_option/0`) and [`exec`](`t:exec_daemon_option/0`) are\n   847→disabled and only subsystem channels are allowed.\n   848→\"\"\".\n   849→-doc(#{title => <<\"Daemon Options\">>}).\n   850→-type ssh_cli_daemon_option()   :: {ssh_cli, mod_args() | no_cli }.\n   851→\n   852→-doc \"\"\"\n   853→Enables (`true`) or disables (`false`) the possibility to tunnel a TCP/IP\n   854→connection out of a [server](`daemon/2`). The Secure Shell (SSH)\n    28→Protocol is a protocol for secure remote login and other secure network services\n    29→over an insecure network. See [ssh](ssh_app.md#supported) for details of\n    30→supported RFCs, versions, algorithms and unicode handling.\n    31→\n    32→With the SSH application it is possible to start _clients_ and to start\n    33→_daemons_ (servers).\n    34→\n    35→Clients are started with `connect/2`, `connect/3` or `connect/4`. They open an\n    36→encrypted connection on top of TCP/IP. In that encrypted connection one or more\n    37→channels could be opened with\n    38→[ssh_connection:session_channel/2,4](`ssh_connection:session_channel/2`).\n    39→\n    40→Each channel is an isolated \"pipe\" between a client-side process and a\n    41→server-side process. Those process pairs could handle for example file transfers\n    42→(sftp) or remote command execution (shell, exec and/or cli). If a custom shell\n    43→is implemented, the user of the client could execute the special commands\n    44→remotely. Note that the user is not necessarily a human but probably a system\n    45→interfacing the SSH app.\n    46→\n    47→A server-side subssystem (channel) server is requested by the client with\n    48→`ssh_connection:subsystem/4`.\n    49→\n    50→A server (daemon) is started with [daemon/1](`daemon/2`), `daemon/2` or\n    51→[daemon/3](`daemon/2`). Possible channel handlers (subsystems) are declared with\n    52→the [subsystem](`t:subsystem_daemon_option/0`) option when the daemon is\n    53→started.\n    54→\n    55→To just run a shell on a remote machine, there are functions that bundles the\n    56→needed three steps needed into one: [shell/1,2,3](`shell/1`). Similarly, to just\n    57→open an sftp (file transfer) connection to a remote machine, the simplest way is\n    58→to use [ssh_sftp:start_channel/1,2,3](`ssh_sftp:start_channel/1`).\n    59→\n    60→To write your own client channel handler, use the behaviour\n    61→`m:ssh_client_channel`. For server channel handlers use `m:ssh_server_channel`\n    62→behaviour (replaces ssh_daemon_channel).\n    63→\n    64→Both clients and daemons accept options that control the exact behaviour. Some\n    65→options are common to both. The three sets are called\n    66→[Client Options](`t:client_options/0`), [Daemon Options](`t:daemon_options/0`)\n    67→and [Common Options](`t:common_options/0`).\n    68→\n    69→The descriptions of the options uses the\n    70→[Erlang Type Language](`e:system:typespec.md`) with explaining text.\n    71→\n    72→> #### Note {: .info }\n    73→>\n    74→> See also [SSH Application Reference](index.html) and [Examples](using_ssh.md) section.\n    75→\n    76→## Keys and files\n    77→\n    78→A number of objects must be present for the SSH application to work. Those\n    79→objects are per default stored in files. The default names, paths and file\n    80→formats are the same as for [OpenSSH](http://www.openssh.com). Keys could be\n    81→generated with the `ssh-keygen` program from OpenSSH. See the\n    82→[User's Guide](using_ssh.md#running-an-erlang-ssh-daemon).\n    83→\n    84→The paths could easily be changed by options:\n    85→[`user_dir`](`t:ssh_file:user_dir_common_option/0`) and\n    86→[`system_dir`](`t:ssh_file:system_dir_daemon_option/0`).\n    87→\n    88→A completely different storage could be interfaced by writing callback modules\n    89→using the behaviours `m:ssh_client_key_api` and/or `m:ssh_server_key_api`. A\n    90→callback module is installed with the option\n    91→[`key_cb`](`t:key_cb_common_option/0`) to the client and/or the daemon.\n    92→\n    93→### Daemons\n    94→\n    95→The keys are by default stored in files:\n    96→\n    97→- Mandatory: one or more _Host key(s)_, both private and public. Default is to\n    98→  store them in the directory `/etc/ssh` in the files\n    99→\n   100→  - `ssh_host_dsa_key` and `ssh_host_dsa_key.pub`\n   101→  - `ssh_host_rsa_key` and `ssh_host_rsa_key.pub`\n   102→  - `ssh_host_ecdsa_key` and `ssh_host_ecdsa_key.pub`\n   103→\n   104→  The host keys directory could be changed with the option\n   105→  [`system_dir`](`t:ssh_file:system_dir_daemon_option/0`).\n   106→\n   107→- Optional: one or more _User's public key_ in case of `publickey`\n   108→  authorization. Default is to store them concatenated in the file\n   109→  `.ssh/authorized_keys` in the user's home directory.\n   110→\n   111→  The user keys directory could be changed with the option\n   112→  [`user_dir`](`t:ssh_file:user_dir_common_option/0`).\n   113→\n   114→### Clients\n   115→\n   116→The keys and some other data are by default stored in files in the directory\n   117→`.ssh` in the user's home directory.\n   118→\n   119→The directory could be changed with the option\n   120→[`user_dir`](`t:ssh_file:user_dir_common_option/0`).\n   121→\n   122→- Optional: a list of _Host public key(s)_ for previously connected hosts. This\n   123→  list is handled by the SSH application without any need of user assistance.\n   124→  The default is to store them in the file `known_hosts`.\n   125→\n   126→  The `t:host_accepting_client_options/0` are associated with this list of keys.\n   127→\n   128→- Optional: one or more _User's private key(s)_ in case of `publickey`\n   129→  authorization. The default files are\n   130→  - `id_dsa` and `id_dsa.pub`\n   131→  - `id_rsa` and `id_rsa.pub`\n   132→  - `id_ecdsa` and `id_ecdsa.pub`\n   133→\"\"\".\n   134→-moduledoc(#{titles =>\n   135→                 [{type,<<\"Client Options\">>},\n   136→                  {type,<<\"Daemon Options\">>},\n   137→                  {type,<<\"Common Options\">>},\n   138→                  {type,<<\"Other data types\">>}]}).\n   139→\n   140→-include(\"ssh.hrl\").\n   141→-include(\"ssh_connect.hrl\").\n   142→-include_lib(\"public_key/include/public_key.hrl\").\n   143→-include_lib(\"kernel/include/file.hrl\").\n   144→-include_lib(\"kernel/include/inet.hrl\").\n   145→\n   146→-export([start/0, start/1, stop/0,\n   147→\t connect/2, connect/3, connect/4,\n   148→\t close/1, connection_info/2,\n   149→         connection_info/1,\n   150→\t channel_info/3,\n\n<system-reminder>\nWhenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware. Command Execution & Channel Management**\n- `/app/workspace/otp_src_27.3.2/lib/ssh/src/ssh_cli.erl` (1,009 lines)\n  - **SSH CLI channel implementation** - implements `ssh_server_channel` behavior\n  - Handles shell and command execution\n  - Key message handlers:\n    - `handle_ssh_msg/2` callback processes channel events\n    - `{shell, ChannelId, WantReply}` → calls `start_shell/2`\n    - `{exec, ChannelId, WantReply, Cmd0}` → command execution paths\n  - Execution paths:\n    - `exec_direct/5` - Direct function/MFA execution\n    - `exec_in_erlang_default_shell/5` - Erlang shell execution\n    - `exec_in_self_group/5` - Group-based execution\n    - `start_exec_shell/3` - Custom shell execution\n  - Exit status reporting via `ssh_connection:exit_status/3`\n\n- `/app/workspace/otp_src_27.3.2/lib/ssh/src/ssh_connection.erl` (74,336 bytes)\n  - **Connection protocol implementation** (RFC 4254)\n  - Channel request handling\n  - Exports: `exec/4`, `shell/2`, `subsystem/4`, `send/3,4,5`\n  - Key message: `SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST` (type 98)\n  - Routes requests to appropriate channel handlers\n\n- `/app/workspace/otp_src_27.3.2/lib/ssh/src/ssh_server_channel.erl`\n  - Behavior module for server-side channel implementations\n  - Defines callback interface: `init/1`, `handle_ssh_msg/2`, `handle_msg/2`, `terminate/2`\n\n#### **F. SSH MESSAGE TYPES\n\n**Authentication Messages** (from ssh_auth.hrl):\n- `SSH_MSG_USERAUTH_REQUEST` = 50\n- `SSH_MSG_USERAUTH_FAILURE` = 51\n- `SSH_MSG_USERAUTH_SUCCESS` = 52\n- `SSH_MSG_USERAUTH_INFO_REQUEST` = 60\n- `SSH_MSG_USERAUTH_INFO_RESPONSE` = 61\n\n**Connection Messages** (from ssh_connect.hrl):\n- `SSH_MSG_GLOBAL_REQUEST` = 80\n- `SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_OPEN` = 90\n- `SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_OPEN_CONFIRMATION` = 91\n- `SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_DATA` = 94\n- `SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST` = 98 (handles \"exec\", \"shell\", \"subsystem\")\n- `SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_SUCCESS` = 99\n- `SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_FAILURE` = 100\n\n**Transport Messages** (from ssh_transport.hrl):\n- `SSH_MSG_DISCONNECT` = 1\n- `SSH_MSG_SERVICE_REQUEST` = 5\n- `SSH_MSG_SERVICE_ACCEPT` = 6\n- `SSH_MSG_KEXINIT` = 20\n\n### 4. ?UINT32(Sz1), OldBinPwd:Sz1/binary,\n   258→\t\t\t\t\t\t\t   %% ?UINT32(Sz2), NewBinPwd:Sz2/binary\n   259→\t\t\t\t\t\t\t >>\n   260→\t\t\t\t\t\t }, _, \n   261→\t\t\t#ssh{userauth_supported_methods = Methods} = Ssh) ->\n   262→    %% Password change without us having sent SSH_MSG_USERAUTH_PASSWD_CHANGEREQ (because we never do)\n   263→    %% RFC 4252 says:\n   264→    %%   SSH_MSG_USERAUTH_FAILURE without partial success - The password\n   265→    %%   has not been changed.  Either password changing was not supported,\n   266→    %%   or the old password was bad. \n   267→\n   268→    {not_authorized, {User, {error,\"Password change not supported\"}}, \n   269→     {#ssh_msg_userauth_failure{authentications = Methods,\n   270→                                partial_success = false}, Ssh}\n   271→    };\n   272→\n   273→handle_userauth_request(#ssh_msg_userauth_request{user = User,\n   274→\t\t\t\t\t\t  service = \"ssh-connection\",\n   275→\t\t\t\t\t\t  method = \"none\"}, _,\n   276→\t\t\t#ssh{userauth_supported_methods = Methods,\n   277→                             opts = Opts} = Ssh) ->\n   278→    case ?GET_OPT(no_auth_needed, Opts) of\n   279→        false ->\n   280→            %% The normal case\n   281→            {not_authorized, {User, undefined},\n   282→             {#ssh_msg_userauth_failure{authentications = Methods,\n   283→                                        partial_success = false}, Ssh}\n   284→            };\n   285→        true ->\n   286→            %% RFC 4252  5.2\n   287→\t    {authorized, User,\n   288→             {#ssh_msg_userauth_success{}, Ssh}\n   289→            }\n   290→    end;\n   291→\n   292→handle_userauth_request(#ssh_msg_userauth_request{user = User,\n   293→\t\t\t\t\t\t  service = \"ssh-connection\",\n   294→\t\t\t\t\t\t  method = \"publickey\",\n   295→\t\t\t\t\t\t  data = <<?BYTE(?FALSE),\n   296→\t\t\t\t\t\t\t   ?UINT32(ALen), BAlg:ALen/binary,\n   297→\t\t\t\t\t\t\t   ?UINT32(KLen), KeyBlob:KLen/binary,\n   298→\t\t\t\t\t\t\t   _/binary\n   299→\t\t\t\t\t\t\t >>\n   300→\t\t\t\t\t\t }, \n   301→\t\t\t_SessionId, \n   302→\t\t\t#ssh{userauth_supported_methods = Methods} = Ssh0) ->\n   303→    Ssh =\n   304→        case check_user(User, Ssh0) of\n   305→            {true,Ssh01} -> Ssh01#ssh{user=User};\n   306→            {false,Ssh01} -> Ssh01#ssh{user=false}\n   307→        end,\n   308→\n   309→    case\n   310→        pre_verify_sig(User, KeyBlob, Ssh)\n   311→    of\n   312→\ttrue ->\n   313→\t    {not_authorized, {User, undefined},\n   314→             {#ssh_msg_userauth_pk_ok{algorithm_name = binary_to_list(BAlg),\n   315→                                     key_blob = KeyBlob}, Ssh}\n   316→            };\n   317→\tfalse ->\n   318→\t    {not_authorized, {User, undefined}, \n   319→\t     {#ssh_msg_userauth_failure{authentications = Methods,\n   320→                                        partial_success = false}, Ssh}\n   321→            }\n   322→    end;\n   323→\n   324→handle_userauth_request(#ssh_msg_userauth_request{user = User,\n   325→\t\t\t\t\t\t  service = \"ssh-connection\",\n   326→\t\t\t\t\t\t  method = \"publickey\",\n   327→\t\t\t\t\t\t  data = <<?BYTE(?TRUE),\n   328→\t\t\t\t\t\t\t   ?UINT32(ALen), BAlg:ALen/binary,\n   329→\t\t\t\t\t\t\t   ?UINT32(KLen), KeyBlob:KLen/binary,\n   330→\t\t\t\t\t\t\t   SigWLen/binary>>\n   331→\t\t\t\t\t\t }, \n   332→\t\t\tSessionId, \n   333→\t\t\t#ssh{user = PreVerifyUser,\n   334→                             userauth_supported_methods = Methods} = Ssh0) ->\n   335→    \n   336→    {UserOk,Ssh} = check_user(User, Ssh0),\n   337→    case\n   338→        ((PreVerifyUser == User) orelse (PreVerifyUser == undefined)) andalso\n   339→        UserOk andalso\n   340→        verify_sig(SessionId, User, \"ssh-connection\", BAlg, KeyBlob, SigWLen, Ssh)\n   341→    of\n   342→\ttrue ->\n   343→\t    {authorized, User, \n   344→             {#ssh_msg_userauth_success{}, Ssh}\n   345→            };\n   346→\tfalse ->\n   347→\t    {not_authorized, {User, undefined}, \n   348→\t     {#ssh_msg_userauth_failure{authentications = Methods,\n   349→                                        partial_success = false}, Ssh}\n   350→            }\n   351→    end;\n   352→\n   353→handle_userauth_request(#ssh_msg_userauth_request{user = User,\n   354→\t\t\t\t\t\t  service = \"ssh-connection\",\n   355→\t\t\t\t\t\t  method = \"keyboard-interactive\",\n   356→\t\t\t\t\t\t  data = _},\n   357→\t\t\t_, #ssh{opts = Opts,\n   358→\t\t\t\tkb_tries_left = KbTriesLeft,\n   359→\t\t\t\tuserauth_supported_methods = Methods} = Ssh) ->\n   360→    case KbTriesLeft of\n   361→\tN when N<1 ->\n   362→\t    {not_authorized, {User, {authmethod, \"keyboard-interactive\"}}, \n   363→             {#ssh_msg_userauth_failure{authentications = Methods,\n   364→                                        partial_success = false}, Ssh}\n   365→            };\n   366→\n   367→\t_ ->\n   368→\t    %% RFC4256\n   369→\t    %% The data field contains:\n   370→\t    %%   - language tag (deprecated). If =/=[] SHOULD use it however. We skip\n   371→\t    %%                                it for simplicity.\n   372→\t    %%   - submethods. \"... the user can give a hint of which actual methods\n   373→\t    %%                  he wants to use. ...\".  It's a \"MAY use\" so we skip\n   374→\t    %%                  it. It also needs an understanding between the client\n   375→\t    %%                  and the server.\n   376→\t    %%                  \n   377→\t    %% \"The server MUST reply with an SSH_MSG_USERAUTH_SUCCESS,\n   378→\t    %%  SSH_MSG_USERAUTH_FAILURE, or SSH_MSG_USERAUTH_INFO_REQUEST message.\"\n   379→\t    Default = {\"SSH server\",\n   380→\t\t       \"Enter password for \\\"\"++User++\"\\\"\",\n   381→\t\t       \"password: \",\n   382→\t\t       false},\n   383→\n   384→\t    {Name, Instruction, Prompt, Echo} =\n   385→\t\tcase ?GET_OPT(auth_method_kb_interactive_data, Opts) of\n   386→\t\t    undefined -> \n   387→\t\t\tDefault;\n   388→\t\t    {_,_,_,_}=V -> \n   389→\t\t\tV;\n   390→                    F when is_function(F, 4) ->\n   391→\t\t\t{_,PeerName} = Ssh#ssh.peer,\n   392→\t\t\tF(PeerName, User, \"ssh-connection\", Ssh#ssh.pwdfun_user_state);\n   393→\t\t    F when is_function(F) ->\n   394→\t\t\t{_,PeerName} = Ssh#ssh.peer,\n   395→\t\t\tF(PeerName, User, \"ssh-connection\")\n   396→\t\tend,\n   397→\t    EchoEnc = case Echo of\n   398→\t\t\t  true -> <<?TRUE>>;\n   399→\t\t\t  false -> <<?FALSE>>\n   400→\t\t      end,\n   401→\t    Msg = #ssh_msg_userauth_info_request{name = unicode:characters_to_list(Name),\n   402→\t\t\t\t\t\t instruction = unicode:characters_to_list(Instruction),\n   403→\t\t\t\t\t\t language_tag = \"\",\n   404→\t\t\t\t\t\t num_prompts = 1,\n   405→\t\t\t\t\t\t data = <<?STRING(unicode:characters_to_binary(Prompt)),\n   406→\t\t\t\t\t\t\t  EchoEnc/binary\n   407→\t\t\t\t\t\t\t>>\n   408→\t\t\t\t\t\t},\n   409→\t    {not_authorized, {User, undefined}, \n   410→\t     {Msg, Ssh#ssh{user = User}}\n   411→            }\n   412→    end;\n   413→\n   414→handle_userauth_request(#ssh_msg_userauth_request{user = User,\n   415→\t\t\t\t\t\t  service = \"ssh-connection\",\n   416→\t\t\t\t\t\t  method = Other}, _,\n   417→\t\t\t#ssh{userauth_supported_methods = Methods} = Ssh) ->\n   418→    {not_authorized, {User, {authmethod, Other}}, \n   419→     {#ssh_msg_userauth_failure{authentications = Methods,\n   420→                                partial_success = false}, Ssh}\n   421→    }.\n   422→\n   423→\n   424→%%%----------------------------------------------------------------\n   425→%%% keyboard-interactive client\n   426→handle_userauth_info_request(#ssh_msg_userauth_info_request{name = Name,\n   427→\t\t\t\t\t\t\t    instruction = Instr,\n   428→\t\t\t\t\t\t\t    num_prompts = NumPrompts,\n   429→\t\t\t\t\t\t\t    data  = Data},\n   430→\t\t\t     #ssh{opts=Opts} = Ssh) ->\n   431→    PromptInfos = decode_keyboard_interactive_prompts(NumPrompts,Data),\n   432→    case keyboard_interact_get_responses(Opts, Name, Instr, PromptInfos) of\n   433→\tnot_ok ->\n   434→\t    not_ok;\n   435→\tResponses ->\n   436→\t    {ok, \n   437→\t     {#ssh_msg_userauth_info_response{num_responses = NumPrompts,\n   438→                                              data = Responses},\n   439→              Ssh}}\n   440→    end.\n   441→\n   442→%%%----------------------------------------------------------------\n   443→%%% keyboard-interactive server\n   444→handle_userauth_info_response(#ssh_msg_userauth_info_response{num_responses = 1,\n   445→\t\t\t\t\t\t\t      data = <<?UINT32(Sz), Password:Sz/binary>>},\n   446→\t\t\t      #ssh{opts = Opts,\n   447→\t\t\t\t   kb_tries_left = KbTriesLeft,\n   448→\t\t\t\t   user = User,\n   449→\t\t\t\t   userauth_supported_methods = Methods} = Ssh) ->\n   450→    SendOneEmpty =\n   451→\t(?GET_OPT(tstflg,Opts) == one_empty)\n   452→\torelse \n   453→\tproplists:get_value(one_empty, ?GET_OPT(tstflg,Opts), false),\n   454→\n   455→    case check_password(User, unicode:characters_to_list(Password), Ssh) of\n   456→\t{true,Ssh1} when SendOneEmpty==true ->\n   457→\t    {authorized_but_one_more, User,\n   458→             {#ssh_msg_userauth_info_request{name = \"\",\n   459→                                             instruction = \"\",\n   460→                                             language_tag = \"\",\n   461→                                             num_prompts = 0,\n   462→                                             data = <<?BOOLEAN(?FALSE)>>\n   463→                                            },\n   464→              Ssh1}};\n   465→\n   466→\t{true,Ssh1} ->\n   467→\t    {authorized, User,\n   468→\t     {#ssh_msg_userauth_success{}, Ssh1}};\n   469→\n   470→\t{false,Ssh1} ->\n   471→\t    {not_authorized, {User, {error,\"Bad user or password\"}}, \n   472→\t     {#ssh_msg_userauth_failure{authentications = Methods,\n   473→                                        partial_success = false}, \n   474→              Ssh1#ssh{kb_tries_left = max(KbTriesLeft-1, 0)}}}\n   475→    end;\n   476→\n   477→handle_userauth_info_response({extra,#ssh_msg_userauth_info_response{}},\n   478→\t\t\t      #ssh{user = User} = Ssh) ->\n   479→    {authorized, User,\n   480→     {#ssh_msg_userauth_success{}, Ssh}};\n   481→\n   482→handle_userauth_info_response(#ssh_msg_userauth_info_response{},\n   483→\t\t\t      _Auth) ->\n   484→    ?DISCONNECT(?SSH_DISCONNECT_SERVICE_NOT_AVAILABLE,\n   485→                \"Server does not support keyboard-interactive\").\n   486→\n   487→%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n   488→%%% Internal functions\n   489→%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n   490→method_preference(SigKeyAlgs) ->\n   491→    %% PubKeyAlgs: List of user (client) public key algorithms to try to use.\n   492→    %% All of the acceptable algorithms is the default values.\n   493→    PubKeyDefs = [{\"publickey\", ?MODULE, publickey_msg, [A]} || A <- SigKeyAlgs],\n   494→    NonPKmethods = [{\"password\", ?MODULE, password_msg, []},\n   495→                    {\"keyboard-interactive\", ?MODULE, keyboard_interactive_msg, []}\n   496→                   ],\n   497→    PubKeyDefs ++ NonPKmethods.\n   498→\n   499→check_user(User, Ssh) ->\n   500→    case ?GET_OPT(pk_check_user, Ssh#ssh.opts) of\n   501→        true ->\n   502→            check_password(User, pubkey, Ssh);\n   503→        _ ->\n   504→            {true, Ssh} % i.e, skip the test\n   505→    end.\n   506→\n   507→check_password(User, Password, #ssh{opts=Opts} = Ssh) ->\n   508→    case ?GET_OPT(pwdfun, Opts) of\n   509→        undefined when Password==pubkey ->\n   510→            %% Just check the User name\n   511→            case lists:keysearch(User, 1, ?GET_OPT(user_passwords,Opts)) of\n   512→                {value, {User, _}} -> {true, Ssh};\n   513→                false -> {false, Ssh}\n   514→            end;\n   515→\n   516→\tundefined ->\n   517→\t    Static = get_password_option(Opts, User),\n   518→\t    {ssh_lib:comp(Password,Static), Ssh};\n   519→\n   520→\tChecker when is_function(Checker,2) ->\n   521→\t    {Checker(User, Password), Ssh};\n   522→\n   523→\tChecker when is_function(Checker,4) ->\n   524→\t    #ssh{pwdfun_user_state = PrivateState,\n   525→\t\t peer = {_,PeerAddr={_,_}}\n   526→\t\t} = Ssh,\n   527→\t    case Checker(User, Password, PeerAddr, PrivateState) of\n   528→\t\ttrue ->\n   529→\t\t    {true,Ssh};\n   530→\t\tfalse ->\n   531→\t\t    {false,Ssh};\n   532→\t\t{true,NewState} ->\n   533→\t\t    {true, Ssh#ssh{pwdfun_user_state=NewState}};\n   534→\t\t{false,NewState} ->\n   535→\t\t    {false, Ssh#ssh{pwdfun_user_state=NewState}};\n   536→\t\tdisconnect ->\n   537→\t\t    ?DISCONNECT(?SSH_DISCONNECT_NO_MORE_AUTH_METHODS_AVAILABLE,\n   538→                                \"\")\n   539→\t    end\n   540→    end.\n   541→\n   542→get_password_option(Opts, User) ->\n   543→    Passwords = ?GET_OPT(user_passwords, Opts),\n   544→    case lists:keysearch(User, 1, Passwords) of\n   545→\t{value, {User, Pw}} -> Pw;\n   546→\tfalse -> ?GET_OPT(password, Opts)\n   547→    end.\n   548→\t    \n   549→pre_verify_sig(User, KeyBlob,  #ssh{opts=Opts}) ->\n   550→    try\n   551→\tKey = ssh_message:ssh2_pubkey_decode(KeyBlob), % or exception\n   552→        ssh_transport:call_KeyCb(is_auth_key, [Key, User], Opts)\n   553→    catch\n   554→\t_:_ ->\n   555→\t    false\n   556→    end.\n   557→\n   558→verify_sig(SessionId, User, Service, AlgBin, KeyBlob, SigWLen, #ssh{opts=Opts} = Ssh) ->\n   559→    try\n   560→        Alg = binary_to_list(AlgBin),\n   561→        true = lists:member(list_to_existing_atom(Alg), \n   562→                            proplists:get_value(public_key,\n   563→                                                ?GET_OPT(preferred_algorithms,Opts))),\n   564→        Key = ssh_message:ssh2_pubkey_decode(KeyBlob), % or exception\n   565→        true = ssh_transport:call_KeyCb(is_auth_key, [Key, User], Opts),\n   566→        PlainText = build_sig_data(SessionId, User, Service, KeyBlob, Alg),\n   567→        <<?UINT32(AlgSigLen), AlgSig:AlgSigLen/binary>> = SigWLen,\n   568→        <<?UINT32(AlgLen), _Alg:AlgLen/binary,\n   569→          ?UINT32(SigLen), Sig:SigLen/binary>> = AlgSig,\n   570→        ssh_transport:verify(PlainText, list_to_existing_atom(Alg), Sig, Key, Ssh)\n   571→    catch\n   572→\t_:_ ->\n   573→\t    false\n   574→    end.\n   575→\n   576→build_sig_data(SessionId, User, Service, KeyBlob, Alg) ->\n   577→    Sig = [?binary(SessionId),\n   578→\t   ?SSH_MSG_USERAUTH_REQUEST,\n   579→\t   ?string_utf8(User),\n   580→\t   ?string(Service),\n   581→\t   ?binary(<<\"publickey\">>),\n   582→\t   ?TRUE,\n   583→\t   ?string(Alg),\n   584→\t   ?binary(KeyBlob)],\n   585→    list_to_binary(Sig).\n   586→\n   587→\n   588→\n   589→key_alg('rsa-sha2-256') -> 'ssh-rsa';\n   590→key_alg('rsa-sha2-512') -> 'ssh-rsa';\n   591→key_alg(Alg) -> Alg.\n   592→\n   593→%%%================================================================\n   594→%%%\n   595→%%% Keyboard-interactive\n   596→%%% \n   597→\n   598→decode_keyboard_interactive_prompts(_NumPrompts, Data) ->\n   599→    ssh_message:decode_keyboard_interactive_prompts(Data, []).\n   600→\n   601→keyboard_interact_get_responses(Opts, Name, Instr, PromptInfos) ->\n   602→    keyboard_interact_get_responses(?GET_OPT(user_interaction, Opts),\n   603→\t\t\t\t    ?GET_OPT(keyboard_interact_fun, Opts),\n   604→\t\t\t\t    ?GET_OPT(password, Opts),\n   605→                                    Name,\n   606→\t\t\t\t    Instr,\n   607→                                    PromptInfos,\n   608→                                    Opts).\n   609→\n   610→\n   611→%% Don't re-try an already rejected password. This could happen if both keyboard-interactive\n   612→%% and password methods are tried:\n   613→keyboard_interact_get_responses(_, _, not_ok, _, _, _, _) ->\n   614→    not_ok;\n   615→\n   616→%% Only one password requestedm and we have got one via the 'password' option for the daemon:\n   617→keyboard_interact_get_responses(_, undefined, Pwd, _, _, [_], _) when Pwd =/= undefined ->\n   618→    [Pwd]; %% Password auth implemented with keyboard-interaction and passwd is known\n   619→\n   620→%% No password requested (keyboard-interactive):\n   621→keyboard_interact_get_responses(_, _, _, _, _, [], _)  ->\n   622→    [];\n   623→\n   624→%% user_interaction is forbidden (by option user_interaction) and we have to ask\n   625→%% the user for one or more.\n   626→%% Throw an error:\n   627→keyboard_interact_get_responses(false, undefined, undefined, _, _, [Prompt|_], Opts) ->\n   628→    ssh_no_io:read_line(Prompt, Opts);\n   629→\n   630→%% One or more passwords are requested, we may prompt the user and no fun is used\n   631→%% to get the responses:\n   632→keyboard_interact_get_responses(true, undefined, _, Name, Instr, PromptInfos, Opts) ->\n   633→    prompt_user_for_passwords(Name, Instr, PromptInfos, Opts);\n   634→\n   635→%% The passwords are provided with a fun. Use that one!\n   636→keyboard_interact_get_responses(true, Fun, _Pwd, Name, Instr, PromptInfos, _Opts) ->\n   637→    keyboard_interact_fun(Fun, Name, Instr, PromptInfos).\n   638→\n   639→\n   640→\n   641→prompt_user_for_passwords(Name, Instr, PromptInfos, Opts) ->\n   642→    IoCb = ?GET_INTERNAL_OPT(io_cb, Opts),\n   643→    write_if_nonempty(IoCb, Name),\n   644→    write_if_nonempty(IoCb, Instr),\n   645→    lists:map(fun({Prompt, true})  -> IoCb:read_line(Prompt, Opts);\n   646→\t\t ({Prompt, false}) -> IoCb:read_password(Prompt, Opts)\n   647→\t      end,\n   648→\t      PromptInfos).\n   649→\n   650→keyboard_interact_fun(KbdInteractFun, Name, Instr,  PromptInfos) ->\n   651→    case KbdInteractFun(Name, Instr, PromptInfos) of\n   652→\tResponses when is_list(Responses),\n   653→                     length(Responses) == length(PromptInfos) ->\n   654→\t    Responses;\n   655→\t_ ->\n   656→            nok\n   657→    end.\n   658→\n   659→\n   660→write_if_nonempty(_, \"\") -> ok;\n   661→write_if_nonempty(_, <<>>) -> ok;\n   662→write_if_nonempty(IoCb, Text) -> IoCb:format(\"~s~n\",[Text]).\n   663→\n   664→%%%----------------------------------------------------------------\n   665→%%% Called just for the tracer ssh_dbg\n   666→ssh_msg_userauth_result(_R) -> ok.\n   667→\n   668→%%%################################################################\n   669→%%%#\n   670→%%%# Tracing\n   671→%%%#\n   672→\n   673→ssh_dbg_trace_points() -> [authentication].\n   674→\n   675→ssh_dbg_flags(authentication) -> [c].\n   676→\n   677→ssh_dbg_on(authentication) -> dbg:tp(?MODULE, handle_userauth_request, 3, x),\n   678→                              dbg:tp(?MODULE, init_userauth_request_msg, 1, x),\n   679→                              dbg:tp(?MODULE, ssh_msg_userauth_result, 1, x),\n   680→                              dbg:tp(?MODULE, userauth_request_msg, 1, x).\n   681→\n   682→ssh_dbg_off(authentication) -> dbg:ctpg(?MODULE, handle_userauth_request, 3),\n   683→                               dbg:ctpg(?MODULE, init_userauth_request_msg, 1),\n   684→                               dbg:ctpg(?MODULE, ssh_msg_userauth_result, 1),\n   685→                               dbg:ctpg(?MODULE, userauth_request_msg, 1).\n   686→\n   687→\n   688→\n   689→%%% Server ----------------\n   690→ssh_dbg_format(authentication, {call, {?MODULE,handle_userauth_request, [Req,_SessionID,Ssh]}},\n   691→               Stack) ->\n   692→    {skip, [{Req,Ssh}|Stack]};\n   693→\n   694→\n   695→ssh_dbg_format(authentication, {return_from, {?MODULE,handle_userauth_request,3},\n   696→                                {ok,{#ssh_msg_service_accept{name=Name},_Ssh}}},\n   697→               [{#ssh_msg_service_request{name=Name},_} | Stack]) ->\n   698→    {skip, Stack};\n   699→\n   700→ssh_dbg_format(authentication, {return_from, {?MODULE,handle_userauth_request,3},\n   701→                                {authorized,User,_Repl}},\n   702→              [{#ssh_msg_userauth_request{}=Req,Ssh}|Stack]) ->\n   703→    {[\"AUTH srvr: Peer client authorized\\n\",\n   704→      io_lib:format(\"user = ~p~n\", [User]),\n   705→      fmt_req(Req, Ssh)],\n   706→     Stack};\n   707→\n   708→ssh_dbg_format(authentication, {return_from, {?MODULE,handle_userauth_request,3},\n   709→                                {not_authorized,{User,_X},_Repl}},\n   710→               [{#ssh_msg_userauth_request{method=\"none\"},Ssh}|Stack]) ->\n   711→    Methods = API\n%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n%%%----------------------------------------------------------------\nuserauth_request_msg(#ssh{userauth_methods = ServerMethods,\n\t\t\t  userauth_supported_methods = UserPrefMethods,\n\t\t\t  userauth_preference = ClientMethods0\n\t\t\t } = Ssh0) ->\n    case sort_select_mthds(ClientMethods0, UserPrefMethods, \"keyboard-interactive\" -> Ssh0;\n\t\t      _ -> Ssh0#ssh{userauth_preference = Prefs}\n\t\t  end,\n\t    case Module:Function(Args ++ [Ssh]) of\n\t\t{not_ok, Ssh1} ->\n\t\t expressed an opinion via option \"auth_methods\", use the server's prefs\n    sort_select_mthds1(Clients, Servers, string:tokens(?SUPPORTED_AUTH_METHODS,\",\"));\n\nsort_select_mthds(Clients, Users0, Servers0) ->\n    %% The User has an opinion, use the intersection of that and the Servers whishes but\n    %% in the Users order\n    sort_select_mthds1(Clients, string:tokens(Users0,\",\"), Servers0).\n\n\nsort_select_mthds1(Clients, Users0, Servers0) ->\n    Servers = unique(Servers0),\n    Users = unique(Users0),\n    [C || Key <- Users,\n\t  lists:member(Key, Servers),\n\t  C <- Clients,\n\t  element(1,C) == Key].\n\nunique(L) -> \n    lists:reverse(\n      lists:foldl(fun(E,Acc) -> \n\t\t\t  case lists:member(E,Acc) of\n\t\t\t      true -> Acc;\n\t\t\t      false -> [E|Acc]\n\t\t\t  end\n\t\t  end, [], L)).\n    \n\n%%%---- userauth_request_msg \"callbacks\"\npassword_msg([#ssh{opts = Opts,\n\t\t   user = User,\n                   service = Service} = Ssh0]) ->\n    IoCb = ?GET_INTERNAL_OPT(io_cb, Opts),\n    {Password,Ssh} = \n\tcase ?GET_OPT(password, Opts) of\n\t    undefined when IoCb == ssh_no_io ->\n\t\t{not_ok, Ssh0};\n\t    undefined -> KeyAlg = key_alg(SigAlg),\n    case ssh_transport:call_KeyCb(user_key, [KeyAlg], Opts) of\n        {ok, {ssh2_pubkey, PubKeyBlob}} ->\n            {ok, {ssh2_pubkey, PubKeyBlob}};\n\n        {ok, PrivKey} ->\n            try\n                %% Check the key - the KeyCb may be a buggy plugin\n                true = ssh_transport:valid_key_sha_alg(private, PrivKey, KeyAlg),\n                Key = ssh_file:extract_public_key(PrivKey),\n                ssh_message:ssh2_pubkey_encode(Key)\n            of\n                PubKeyBlob -> {ok, {PrivKey, PubKeyBlob}}\n            catch\n                _:_ -> \n                    not_ok\n            end;\n\n        _Error ->\n            not_ok\n    end.\n\n\npublickey_msg([SigAlg, #ssh{user = User,\n                            session_id = SessionId,\n                            service = Service,\n                            opts = Opts} = Ssh]) ->\n    case get_public_key(SigAlg, Ssh) of\n        {ok, {_, PubKeyBlob} = Key} ->\n            SigAlgStr = atom_to_list(SigAlg),\n            SigData = build_sig_data(SessionId, User, Service, PubKeyBlob, SigAlgStr),\n\n            SigRes = case Key of\n                         {ssh2_pubkey, PubKeyBlob} ->\n                             {ok, ssh_transport:call_KeyCb(sign, [PubKeyBlob, SigData], Opts)};\n                         {PrivKey, PubKeyBlob} ->\n                             ssh_transport:sign(SigData, SigAlg, PrivKey, Ssh)\n                     end,\n            case SigRes of\n                {ok,Sig} ->\n                    SigBlob = list_to_binary([?string(SigAlgStr),\n                                              ?binary(Sig)]),\n\n                    {#ssh_msg_userauth_request{user = User,\n                   ?string(SigAlgStr),\n                                                       ?binary(PubKeyBlob),\n                                                       ?binary(SigBlob)]},\n                     Ssh};\n                {error,_} ->\n                    {not_ok, Ssh}\n            end;\n\n        _ ->\n            {not_ok, Ssh}\n    end.\n\n%%%----------------------------------------------------------------\nservice_request_msg(Ssh) ->\n    {#ssh_msg_service_request{name = \"ssh-userauth\"},\n     Ssh#ssh{service = {#ssh_msg_userauth_success{}, Ssh1}\n            };\n\t{false,Ssh1}  ->\n\t    {not_authorized, {User, {error,\"Bad user or password\"}}, \n\t     {#ssh_msg_userauth_failure{authentications = \"ssh-connection\",\n\t\t\t\t\t\t  method = \"password\",\n\t\t\t\t\t\t  data = <<?TRUE,\n\t\t\t\t\t\t\t   _/binary\n\t\t\t\t\t\t\t   %% ?UINT32(Sz1), OldBinPwd:Sz1/binary,\n\t\t\t\t\t\t\t   %% The API functions in this module send SSH Connection Protocol\nevents, which are received as messages by the remote channel handling the remote\nchannel. The Erlang format of thoose messages is (see also\n[below](`t:event/0`)):\n\n`{ssh_cm,` `t:ssh:connection_ref/0` `,` `t:channel_msg/0` `}`\n\nIf the `m:ssh_client_channel` behavior is used to This variable\n    47→                            % is for the process of guessing the peer encoding, taylord\n    48→                            % after the behaviour of openssh.  If it says latin1 it is so.\n    49→                            % It there arrives characters encoded in latin1 it is so. Otherwise\n    50→                            % assume utf8 until otherwise is proved.\n    51→\t  group,\n    52→\t  buf,\n    53→\t  shell,\n    54→\t  exec\n    55→\t }).\n    56→\n    57→-define(EXEC_ERROR_STATUS, 255).\n    58→\n    59→%%====================================================================\n    60→%% ssh_server_channel callbacks\n    61→%%====================================================================\n    62→\n    63→%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n    64→%% Function: init(Args) -> {ok, State} \n    65→%%                        \n    66→%% Description: Initiates the CLI\n    67→%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n    68→init([Shell, Exec]) ->\n    69→    {ok, #state{shell = Shell, exec = Exec}};\n    70→init([Shell]) ->\n    71→    {ok, #state{shell = Shell}}.\n    72→\n    73→%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n    74→%% Function: handle_ssh_msg(Args) -> {ok, State} | {stop, ChannelId, State}\n    75→%%                        \n    76→%% Description: Handles channel messages received on the ssh-connection.\n    77→%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n    78→handle_ssh_msg({ssh_cm, _ConnectionHandler,\n    79→\t\t{data, _ChannelId, _Type, Data}}, \n    80→\t       #state{group = Group} = State0) ->\n    81→    {Enc, State} = guess_encoding(Data, State0),\n    82→    List = unicode:characters_to_list(Data, Enc),\n    83→    to_group(List, Group, get_dumb(State#state.pty)),\n    84→    {ok, State};\n    85→\n    86→handle_ssh_msg({ssh_cm, ConnectionHandler,\n    87→\t\t{pty, ChannelId, WantReply, \n    88→\t\t {TermName, Width, Height, PixWidth, PixHeight, Modes}}}, \n    89→\t       State0) ->\n    90→    State = State0#state{pty = \n    91→\t\t\t #ssh_pty{term = TermName,\n    92→\t\t\t\t  width =  not_zero(Width, 80),\n    93→\t\t\t\t  height = not_zero(Height, 24),\n    94→\t\t\t\t  pixel_width = PixWidth,\n    95→\t\t\t\t  pixel_height = PixHeight,\n    96→                  modes = Modes},\n    97→             buf = empty_buf()},\n    98→    set_echo(State),\n    99→    ssh_connection:reply_request(ConnectionHandler, WantReply,\n   100→\t\t\t\t success, ChannelId),\n   101→    {ok, State};\n   102→\n   103→handle_ssh_msg({ssh_cm, ConnectionHandler,\n   104→\t    {env, ChannelId, WantReply, Var, Value}}, State = #state{encoding=Enc0}) ->\n   105→    %% It is not as simple as it sounds to set environment variables\n   106→    %% in the server.\n   107→    %% The (OS) env vars should be per per Channel; otherwise anyone\n   108→    %% could affect anyone other's variables.\n   109→    %% Therefore this clause always return a failure.\n   110→    ssh_connection:reply_request(ConnectionHandler,\n   111→\t\t\t\t WantReply, failure, ChannelId),\n   112→\n   113→    %% https://pubs.opengroup.org/onlinepubs/7908799/xbd/envvar.html\n   114→    %% LANG\n   115→    %%     This variable determines the locale category for native language,\n   116→    %%     local customs and coded character set in the absence of the LC_ALL\n   117→    %%     and other LC_* (LC_COLLATE, LC_CTYPE, LC_MESSAGES, LC_MONETARY,\n   118→    %%     LC_NUMERIC, LC_TIME) environment variables. This can be used by\n   119→    %%     applications to determine the language to use for error messages\n   120→    %%     and instructions, collating sequences, date formats, and so forth. \n   121→    %% LC_ALL\n   122→    %%     This variable determines the values for all locale categories. The\n   123→    %%     value of the LC_ALL environment variable has precedence over any of\n   124→    %%     the other environment variables starting with LC_ (LC_COLLATE,\n   125→    %%     LC_CTYPE, LC_MESSAGES, LC_MONETARY, LC_NUMERIC, LC_TIME) and the\n   126→    %%     LANG environment variable.\n   127→    %% ...\n   128→    %%\n   129→    %%  The values of locale categories are determined by a precedence order;\n   130→    %%  the first condition met below determines the value:\n   131→    %%\n   132→    %%   1. If the LC_ALL environment variable is defined and is not null,\n   133→    %%      the value of LC_ALL is used.\n   134→    %%\n   135→    %%   2. If the LC_* environment variable ( LC_COLLATE, LC_CTYPE, LC_MESSAGES,\n   136→    %%      LC_MONETARY, LC_NUMERIC, LC_TIME) is defined and is not null, the\n   137→    %%      value of the environment variable is used to initialise the category\n   138→    %%      that corresponds to the environment variable.\n   139→    %%\n   140→    %%   3. If the LANG environment variable is defined and is not null, the value\n   141→    %%      of the LANG environment variable is used.\n   142→    %%\n   143→    %%   4. If the LANG environment variable is not set or is set to the empty string,\n   144→    %%      the implementation-dependent default locale is used. \n   145→\n   146→    Enc =\n   147→        %% Rule 1 and 3 above says that LC_ALL has precedence over LANG. Since they\n   148→        %% arrives in different messages and in an undefined order, it is resolved\n   149→        %% like this:\n   150→        case Var of\n   151→            <<\"LANG\">> when Enc0==undefined ->\n   152→                %% No previous LC_ALL\n   153→                case claim_encoding(Value) of\n   154→                    {ok,Enc1} -> Enc1;\n   155→                    _ -> Enc0\n   156→                end;\n   157→            <<\"LC_ALL\">> ->\n   158→                %% Maybe or maybe not a LANG has been handled, LC_ALL doesn't care\n   159→                case claim_encoding(Value) of\n   160→                    {ok,Enc1} -> Enc1;\n   161→                    _ -> Enc0\n   162→                end;\n   163→            _ ->\n   164→                Enc0\n   165→        end,\n   166→    {ok, State#state{encoding=Enc}};\n   167→\n   168→handle_ssh_msg({ssh_cm, ConnectionHandler,\n   169→\t    {window_change, ChannelId, Width, Height, PixWidth, PixHeight}},\n   170→\t   #state{buf = Buf, pty = Pty0} = State) ->\n   171→    Pty = Pty0#ssh_pty{width = Width, height = Height,\n   172→\t\t       pixel_width = PixWidth,\n   173→\t\t       pixel_height = PixHeight},\n   174→    {Chars, NewBuf} = io_request({window_change, Pty0}, Buf, Pty, undefined),\n   175→    write_chars(ConnectionHandler, ChannelId, Chars),\n   176→    {ok, State#state{pty = Pty, buf = NewBuf}};\n   177→\n   178→handle_ssh_msg({ssh_cm, ConnectionHandler,  {shell, ChannelId, WantReply}}, #state{shell=disabled} = State) ->\n   179→    write_chars(ConnectionHandler, ChannelId, 1, \"Prohibited.\"),\n   180→    ssh_connection:reply_request(ConnectionHandler, WantReply, success, ChannelId),\n   181→    ssh_connection:exit_status(ConnectionHandler, ChannelId, ?EXEC_ERROR_STATUS),\n   182→    ssh_connection:send_eof(ConnectionHandler, ChannelId),\n   183→    {stop, ChannelId, State#state{channel = ChannelId, cm = ConnectionHandler}};\n   184→handle_ssh_msg({ssh_cm, ConnectionHandler,  {shell, ChannelId, WantReply}}, State0) ->\n   185→    State = case State0#state.encoding of\n   186→                undefined -> State0#state{encoding = utf8};\n   187→                _-> State0\n   188→            end,\n   189→    NewState = start_shell(ConnectionHandler, State),\n   190→    ssh_connection:reply_request(ConnectionHandler, WantReply, success, ChannelId),\n   191→    {ok, NewState#state{channel = ChannelId,\n   192→\t\t\tcm = ConnectionHandler}};\n   193→\n   194→handle_ssh_msg({ssh_cm, ConnectionHandler,  {exec, ChannelId, WantReply, Cmd0}}, S0) ->\n   195→    {Enc,S1} = guess_encoding(Cmd0, S0),\n   196→    Cmd = unicode:characters_to_list(Cmd0, Enc),\n   197→    case\n   198→        case S1#state.exec of\n   199→            disabled ->\n   200→                {\"Prohibited.\", ?EXEC_ERROR_STATUS, 1};\n   201→\n   202→            {direct,F} ->\n   203→                %% Exec called and a Fun or MFA is defined to use.  The F returns the\n   204→                %% value to return.\n   205→                %% The standard I/O is directed from/to the channel ChannelId.\n   206→                exec_direct(ConnectionHandler, ChannelId, Cmd, F, WantReply, S1);\n   207→\n   208→            undefined when S0#state.shell == ?DEFAULT_SHELL ; \n   209→                           S0#state.shell == disabled ->\n   210→                %% Exec called and the shell is the default shell (= Erlang shell).\n   211→                %% To be exact, eval the term as an Erlang term (but not using the\n   212→                %% ?DEFAULT_SHELL directly). This disables banner, prompts and such.\n   213→                %% The standard I/O is directed from/to the channel ChannelId.\n   214→                exec_in_erlang_default_shell(ConnectionHandler, ChannelId, Cmd, WantReply, S1);\n   215→\n   216→            undefined ->\n   217→                %% Exec called, but the a shell other than the default shell is defined.\n   218→                %% No new exec shell is defined, so don't execute!\n   219→                %% We don't know if it is intended to use the new shell or not.\n   220→                {\"Prohibited.\", ?EXEC_ERROR_STATUS, 1};\n   221→\n   222→            _ ->\n   223→                %% Exec called and a Fun or MFA is defined to use.  The F communicates via\n   224→                %% standard io:write/read.\n   225→                %% Kept for compatibility.\n   226→                S2 = start_exec_shell(ConnectionHandler, Cmd, S1),\n   227→                ssh_connection:reply_request(ConnectionHandler, WantReply, success, ChannelId),\n   228→                {ok, S2}\n   229→        end\n   230→    of\n   231→        {Reply, This variable\n                            % is for the process of guessing the peer encoding, taylord\n                            % after the behaviour of openssh.  If it says latin1 it is so.\n                            % It there arrives characters encoded in latin1 it is so. Otherwise\n                            % assume utf8 until otherwise is proved.\n\t  group,\n\t  buf,\n\t  shell,\n\t  exec\n\t }).\n\n-define(EXEC_ERROR_STATUS, 255).\n\n%%====================================================================\n%% ssh_server_channel callbacks\n%%====================================================================\n\n%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n%% Function: init(Args) -> {ok, State} \n%%                        \n%% Description: Initiates the CLI\n%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\ninit([Shell, Exec]) ->\n    {ok, #state{shell = Shell, exec = Exec}};\ninit([Shell]) ->\n    {ok, #state{shell = Shell}}.\n\n%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n%% Function: handle_ssh_msg(Args) -> {ok, State} | {stop, ChannelId, State}\n%%                        \n%% Description: Handles channel messages received on the ssh-connection.\n%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\nhandle_ssh_msg({ssh_cm, _ConnectionHandler,\n\t\t{data, _ChannelId, _Type, Data}}, \n\t       #state{group = Group} = State0) ->\n    {Enc, State} = guess_encoding(Data, State0),\n    List = unicode:characters_to_list(Data, Enc),\n    to_group(List, Group, get_dumb(State#state.pty)),\n    {ok, State};\n\nhandle_ssh_msg({ssh_cm, ConnectionHandler,\n\t\t{pty, ChannelId, WantReply, \n\t\t {TermName, Width, Height, PixWidth, PixHeight, Modes}}}, \n\t       State0) ->\n    State = State0#state{pty = \n\t\t\t #ssh_pty{term = TermName,\n\t\t\t\t  width =  not_zero(Width, 80),\n\t\t\t\t  height = not_zero(Height, 24),\n\t\t\t\t  pixel_width = PixWidth,\n\t\t\t\t  pixel_height = PixHeight,\n                  modes = Modes},\n             buf = empty_buf()},\n    set_echo(State),\n    ssh_connection:reply_request(ConnectionHandler, WantReply,\n\t\t\t\t success, ChannelId),\n    {ok, State};\n\nhandle_ssh_msg({ssh_cm, ConnectionHandler,\n\t    {env, ChannelId, WantReply, Var, Value}}, State = #state{encoding=Enc0}) ->\n    %% It is not as simple as it sounds to set environment variables\n    %% in the server.\n    %% The (OS) env vars should be per per Channel; otherwise anyone\n    %% could affect anyone other's variables.\n    %% Therefore this clause always return a failure.\n    ssh_connection:reply_request(ConnectionHandler,\n\t\t\t\t WantReply, failure, ChannelId),\n\n    %% https://pubs.opengroup.org/onlinepubs/7908799/xbd/envvar.html\n    %% LANG\n    %%     This variable determines the locale category for native language,\n    %%     local customs and coded character set in the absence of the LC_ALL\n    %%     and other LC_* (LC_COLLATE, LC_CTYPE, LC_MESSAGES, LC_MONETARY,\n    %%     LC_NUMERIC, LC_TIME) environment variables. This can be used by\n    %%     applications to determine the language to use for error messages\n    %%     and instructions, collating sequences, date formats, and so forth. \n    %% LC_ALL\n    %%     This variable determines the values for all locale categories. The\n    %%     value of the LC_ALL environment variable has precedence over any of\n    %%     the other environment variables starting with LC_ (LC_COLLATE,\n    %%     LC_CTYPE, LC_MESSAGES, LC_MONETARY, LC_NUMERIC, LC_TIME) and the\n    %%     LANG environment variable.\n    %% ...\n    %%\n    %%  The values of locale categories are determined by a precedence order;\n    %%  the first condition met below determines the value:\n    %%\n    %%   1. If the LC_ALL environment variable is defined and is not null,\n    %%      the value of LC_ALL is used.\n    %%\n    %%   2. If the LC_* environment variable ( LC_COLLATE, LC_CTYPE, LC_MESSAGES,\n    %%      LC_MONETARY, LC_NUMERIC, LC_TIME) is defined and is not null, the\n    %%      value of the environment variable is used to initialise the category\n    %%      that corresponds to the environment variable.\n    %%\n    %%   3. If the LANG environment variable is defined and is not null, the value\n    %%      of the LANG environment variable is used.\n    %%\n    %%   4. If the LANG environment variable is not set or is set to the empty string,\n    %%      the implementation-dependent default locale is used. \n\n    Enc =\n        %% Rule 1 and 3 above says that LC_ALL has precedence over LANG. Since they\n        %% arrives in different messages and in an undefined order, it is resolved\n        %% like this:\n        case Var of\n            <<\"LANG\">> when Enc0==undefined ->\n                %% No previous LC_ALL\n                case claim_encoding(Value) of\n                    {ok,Enc1} -> Enc1;\n                    _ -> Enc0\n                end;\n            <<\"LC_ALL\">> ->\n                %% Maybe or maybe not a LANG has been handled, LC_ALL doesn't care\n                case claim_encoding(Value) of\n                    {ok,Enc1} -> Enc1;\n                    _ -> Enc0\n                end;\n            _ ->\n                Enc0\n        end,\n    {ok, State#state{encoding=Enc}};\n\nhandle_ssh_msg({ssh_cm, ConnectionHandler,\n\t    {window_change, ChannelId, Width, Height, PixWidth, PixHeight}},\n\t   #state{buf = Buf, pty = Pty0} = State) ->\n    Pty = Pty0#ssh_pty{width = Width, height = Height,\n\t\t       pixel_width = PixWidth,\n\t\t       pixel_height = PixHeight},\n    {Chars, NewBuf} = io_request({window_change, Pty0}, Buf, Pty, undefined),\n    write_chars(ConnectionHandler, ChannelId, Chars),\n    {ok, State#state{pty = Pty, buf = NewBuf}};\n\nhandle_ssh_msg({ssh_cm, ConnectionHandler,  {shell, ChannelId, WantReply}}, #state{shell=disabled} = State) ->\n    write_chars(ConnectionHandler, ChannelId, 1, \"Prohibited.\"),\n    ssh_connection:reply_request(ConnectionHandler, WantReply, success, ChannelId),\n    ssh_connection:exit_status(ConnectionHandler, ChannelId, ?EXEC_ERROR_STATUS),\n    ssh_connection:send_eof(ConnectionHandler, ChannelId),\n    {stop, ChannelId, State#state{channel = ChannelId, cm = ConnectionHandler}};\nhandle_ssh_msg({ssh_cm, ConnectionHandler,  {shell, ChannelId, WantReply}}, State0) ->\n    State = case State0#state.encoding of\n                undefined -> State0#state{encoding = utf8};\n                _-> State0\n            end,\n    NewState = start_shell(ConnectionHandler, State),\n    ssh_connection:reply_request(ConnectionHandler, WantReply, success, ChannelId),\n    {ok, NewState#state{channel = ChannelId,\n\t\t\tcm = ConnectionHandler}};\n\nhandle_ssh_msg({ssh_cm, ConnectionHandler,  {exec, ChannelId, WantReply, Cmd0}}, S0) ->\n    {Enc,S1} = guess_encoding(Cmd0, S0),\n    Cmd = unicode:characters_to_list(Cmd0, Enc),\n    case\n        case S1#state.exec of\n            disabled ->\n                {\"Prohibited.\", ?EXEC_ERROR_STATUS, 1};\n\n            {direct,F} ->\n                %% Exec called and a Fun or MFA is defined to use.  The F returns the\n                %% value to return.\n                %% The standard I/O is directed from/to the channel ChannelId.\n                exec_direct(ConnectionHandler, ChannelId, Cmd, F, WantReply, S1);\n\n            undefined when S0#state.shell == ?DEFAULT_SHELL ; \n                           S0#state.shell == disabled ->\n                %% Exec called and the shell is the default shell (= Erlang shell).\n                %% To be exact, eval the term as an Erlang term (but not using the\n                %% ?DEFAULT_SHELL directly). This disables banner, prompts and such.\n                %% The standard I/O is directed from/to the channel ChannelId.\n                exec_in_erlang_default_shell(ConnectionHandler, ChannelId, Cmd, WantReply, S1);\n\n            undefined ->\n                %% Exec called, but the a shell other than the default shell is defined.\n                %% No new exec shell is defined, so don't execute!\n                %% We don't know if it is intended to use the new shell or not.\n                {\"Prohibited.\", ?EXEC_ERROR_STATUS, 1};\n\n            _ ->\n                %% Exec called and a Fun or MFA is defined to use.  The F communicates via\n                %% standard io:write/read.\n                %% Kept for compatibility.\n                S2 = start_exec_shell(ConnectionHandler, Cmd, S1),\n                ssh_connection:reply_request(ConnectionHandler, WantReply, success, ChannelId),\n                {ok, S2}\n        end\n    of\n        {Reply, Status, Type} ->\n            write_chars(ConnectionHandler, ChannelId, Type,\n                        unicode:characters_to_binary(Reply, utf8, out_enc(S1))),\n            ssh_connection:reply_request(ConnectionHandler, WantReply, success, ChannelId),\n            ssh_connection:exit_status(ConnectionHandler, ChannelId, Status),\n            ssh_connection:send_eof(ConnectionHandler, ChannelId),\n            {stop, ChannelId, S1#state{channel = ChannelId, cm = ConnectionHandler}};\n            \n        {ok, S} ->\n            {ok, S#state{channel = ChannelId,\n                         cm = ConnectionHandler}}\n    end;\n\nhandle_ssh_msg({ssh_cm, _ConnectionHandler, {eof, _ChannelId}}, State) ->\n    {ok, State};\n\nhandle_ssh_msg({ssh_cm, _, {signal, _, _}}, State) ->\n    %% Ignore signals according to RFC 4254 section 6.9.\n    {ok, State};\n\nhandle_ssh_msg({ssh_cm, _, {exit_signal, ChannelId, _, Error, _}}, State) ->\n    Report = io_lib:format(\"Connection closed by peer ~n Error ~p~n\",\n\t\t\t   [Error]),\n    error_logger:error_report(Report),\n    {stop, ChannelId,  State};\n\nhandle_ssh_msg({ssh_cm, _, {exit_status, ChannelId, 0}}, State) ->\n    {stop, ChannelId, State};\n\nhandle_ssh_msg({ssh_cm, _, {exit_status, ChannelId, Status}}, State) ->\n    \n    Report = io_lib:format(\"Connection closed by peer ~n Status ~p~n\",\n\t\t\t   [Status]),\n    error_logger:error_report(Report),\n    {stop, ChannelId, State}.\n\n%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n%% Function: handle_msg(Args) -> {ok, State} | {stop, ChannelId, State}\n%%                        \n%% Description: Handles other channel messages.\n%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\nhandle_msg({ssh_channel_up, call_disconnectfun_and_log_cond(LogMsg, DetailedText, ?MODULE, ?LINE, StateName, D)).\n    97→\n    98→%%====================================================================\n    99→%% Start / stop\n   100→%%====================================================================\n   101→\n   102→start_link(Role, Socket, Options) ->\n   103→    start_link(Role, undefined, Socket, Options).\n   104→\n   105→start_link(Role, Id, Socket, Options) ->\n   106→    case gen_statem:start_link(?MODULE,\n   107→                               [Role, Socket, Options],\n   108→                               [{spawn_opt, [{message_queue_data,off_heap}]}]) of\n   109→\n   110→        {ok, Pid} when Id =/= undefined ->\n   111→            %% Announce the ConnectionRef to the system supervisor so it could\n   112→            %%   1) initiate the socket handover, and\n   113→            %%   2) be returned to whoever called for example ssh:connect; the Pid\n   114→            %%      returned from this function is \"consumed\" by the connection\n   115→            %%      supervisor.\n   116→            ?GET_INTERNAL_OPT(user_pid,Options) ! {new_connection_ref, Id, Pid},\n   117→            {ok, Pid};\n   118→\n   119→        Others ->\n   120→            Others\n   121→    end.\n   122→\n   123→takeover(ConnPid, Role, Socket, Options) ->\n   124→    case Role of\n   125→        client ->\n   126→            group_leader(group_leader(), ConnPid);\n   127→        _ ->\n   128→            ok\n   129→    end,\n   130→    {_, Callback, _} = ?GET_OPT(transport, Options),\n   131→    case Callback:controlling_process(Socket, ConnPid) of\n   132→        ok ->\n   133→            Ref = erlang:monitor(process, ConnPid),\n   134→            gen_statem:cast(ConnPid, socket_control),\n   135→            NegTimeout = ?GET_INTERNAL_OPT(negotiation_timeout,\n   136→                                           Options,\n   137→                                           ?GET_OPT(negotiation_timeout, Options)\n   138→                                          ),\n   139→            handshake(ConnPid, Role, Ref, NegTimeout);\n   140→        {error, Reason}\t->\n   141→            {error, Reason}\n   142→    end.\n   143→\n   144→%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n   145→-spec stop(connection_ref()\n   146→\t  ) -> ok | {error, term()}.\n   147→%% . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . .\n   148→stop(ConnectionHandler)->\n   149→    case call(ConnectionHandler, stop) of\n   150→       {error, closed} ->\n   151→\t    ok;\n   152→\tOther ->\n   153→\t    Other\n   154→    end.\n   155→\n   156→%%====================================================================\n   157→%% Internal application API\n   158→%%====================================================================\n   159→\n   160→%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n   161→%%% Some other module has decided to disconnect.\n   162→\n   163→-spec disconnect(Code::integer(), Details::iodata(),\n   164→                      Module::atom(), Line::integer()) -> no_return().\n   165→%% . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . .\n   166→\n   167→% Preferable called with the macro ?DISCONNECT\n   168→\n   169→disconnect(Code, DetailedText, Module, Line) ->\n   170→    throw({keep_state_and_data,\n   171→\t   [{next_event, internal, {send_disconnect, Code, DetailedText, Module, Line}}]}).\n   172→\n The API functions in this module send SSH Connection Protocol\n    35→events, which are received as messages by the remote channel handling the remote\n    36→channel.       userauth_quiet_mode = ?GET_OPT(quiet_mode, Opts),\n                       peer = {PeerName, PeerAddr},\n                       local = LocalName\n                      },\n receive\n\t{ConnPid, ssh_connected} ->\n\t    erlang:demonitor(Ref, [flush]),\n\t    {ok, ConnPid};\n\t{ConnPid, {not_connected, Reason}} ->\n\t    erlang:demonitor(Ref, [flush]),\n\t    {error, Reason};\n\t{'DOWN', Ref, process, ConnPid, {shutdown, Reason}} ->\n\t    {error, Reason};\n\t{'DOWN', Ref, process, ConnPid, Reason} ->\n\t    {error, Reason};\n        {'EXIT',_,Reason} ->\n            stop(ConnPid),\n            {error, {exit,Reason}}\n    after Timeout ->\n\t    erlang:demonitor(Ref, [flush]),\n\t    ssh_connection_handler:stop(ConnPid),\n\t    {error, timeout}\n    end;\nhandshake(ConnPid, client, Ref, Timeout) ->\n    receive\n\t{ConnPid, ssh_connected} ->\n\t    erlang:demonitor(Ref, [flush]),\n\t    {ok, ConnPid};\n\t{ConnPid, {not_connected, Reason}} ->\n\t    erlang:demonitor(Ref, [flush]),\n\t    {error, Reason};\n\t{'DOWN', Ref, process, ConnPid, {shutdown, Reason}} ->\n\t    {error, Reason};\n\t{'DOWN', Ref, process, ConnPid, Reason} ->\n\t    {error, Reason}\n    after Timeout ->\n\t    erlang:demonitor(Ref, [flush]),\n\t    ssh_connection_handler:stop(ConnPid),\n\t    {error, timeout}\n    end.\n\nhandshake(Msg, #data{starter = User}) ->\n    User ! {self(), Msg}.\n\n%%====================================================================\n%% gen_statem callbacks\n%%====================================================================\n%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n-type event_content() ::  any().\n\n-type     | {key_exchange,              role(), renegotiate_flag()}\n      | {key_exchange_dh_gex_init,  server, renegotiate_flag()}\n      | {key_exchange_dh_gex_reply, client, renegotiate_flag()}\n      | {userauth_keyboard_interactive_info_response, client  }\n      | {connected,                 role()                    }\n\t.\n\n%% The state names must fulfill some rules regarding\n%% where the role() and the renegotiate_flag() is placed:\n\n-spec renegotiation(state_name()) -> boolean().\nrenegotiation({_,_,ReNeg}) -> ReNeg == renegotiate;\nrenegotiation(_) -> false.\n\n\n-define(CONNECTED(StateName),\n        (element(1,StateName) == connected orelse\n         element(1,StateName) == ext_info ) ).\n\n-spec handle_event(gen_statem:event_type(),\n\t\t   event_content(),\n\t\t   state_name(),\n\t\t   #data{}\n\t\t  ) -> gen_statem:event_handler_result(state_name()) .\n\n-define(CONNECTION_MSG(Msg),\n        [{next_event, internal,
